\text{\makebox[0.0pt][r]{$\mathcal{B}^{\hskip 2.2pt\mathbf{{a}}}_{\hskip-1.0pt\mu\nu}(\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}R}}\,\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}S}}|\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.545,0.7451}T}}\,\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}U}})\;\raisebox{-0.95pt}{\scalebox{1.25}{$\Leftrightarrow$}}\;$}}\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{figures/default_basis_four_generic_reps}}}$}\,.\vskip-0.5pt

(4.26)

The details of this basis depend strongly on the Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}
and the representations of particles involved. Although it is reasonably straight-forward to construct these tensors explicitly for any chosen representations, we would have no ‘standard’ or familiar choice of tensors with which we could compare (whether or not they form a ‘basis’). For this reason, it is worthwhile to focus our attention to simpler cases—those involving combinations particles with some fixed representation and adjoints, say. For this, it is worthwhile to specialize further and consider matter charged only in the fundamental or ‘defining’ representation
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}F}}
of the Lie algbera.

Let us first consider the cases where an
odd
number of
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}F}}
-coloured particles are involved. For example, is there ever any support for colour tensors involving 3 fundamental particles and a single adjoint, say? As described in
[
45
]
, this is a simple question of representation theory, and turns out to only have support in the case of gauge theories involving one of six isolated simple Lie algebras:
\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}F}}\,\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}F}}\,\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}F}}|\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}ad}})
is non-empty for the simple Lie algebras
\{\mathfrak{a}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}2}},\mathfrak{b}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}2}},\mathfrak{e}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}6}},\mathfrak{e}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}8}},\mathfrak{f}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}4}},\mathfrak{g}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}2}}\}
, with ranks
\{2,1,2,5,2,2\}
, respectively. Similarly,
\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}F}}\,\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}ad}}|\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}ad}}\,\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}ad}})
is non-empty only for the simple Lie algebras
\{\mathfrak{b}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}2}},\mathfrak{b}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}3}},\mathfrak{e}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}8}},\mathfrak{g}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}2}}\}
. Of course, for
\mathfrak{e}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}8}}
, we take the fundamental to be the same as the adjoint, and so the case is fairly exceptional.

4.3.1
Clebsch Colour Bases for
C(\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}F}}\,\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}F}}|\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}F}}\,\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}F}})

Let us begin our analysis with the case of four particles all of which are coloured in the fundamental representation of some simple Lie algebra. In our language, we can span the complete space of colour tensors by sewing together Clebsch-Gordan factors according to:

\text{\makebox[0.0pt][r]{$\mathcal{B}^{\hskip 1.0pt\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}q}}}(\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}F}}\,\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}F}}|\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}F}}\,\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}F}})\;\raisebox{-0.95pt}{\scalebox{1.25}{$\Leftrightarrow$}}\;$}}\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{figures/FFFF_basis_1}}}$}\,.\vskip-0.5pt

(4.27)

As
m{\hskip 0.0pt}^{\smash{\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}F}}\,\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}F}}}}_{\phantom{\smash{\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}F}}\,\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}F}}}}\smash{\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}q}}}}\!=\!1
for the fundamental representations of all simple Lie algebras as we have defined them (see
appendix
B
), each tensor is uniquely labelled by the irreducible representation
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}q}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}i}}
appearing in the tensor product representation’s decomposition

\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}F}}\!\otimes\!\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}F}}\text{\makebox[14.5pt][c]{$\hskip-150.0pt\displaystyle\text{\makebox[0.0pt][r]{$$}}=\text{\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\raisebox{0.47pt}{\hskip-4.0pt:\hskip 1.25pt}$}}\hskip-150.0pt$}}\bigoplus_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}i}}\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}q}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}i}}\,.\vskip-0.5pt

(4.28)

It is easy to see that
every
representation
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}q}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}i}}
appearing in (
4.28
) contributes, and so the number of independent colour tensors for
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}F}}\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}F}}\!\to\!\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}F}}\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}F}}
scattering is counted simply by the number of such irreducible representations.

Table 1
:
Irreducible representations appearing in the decomposition of the tensor product
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}F}}\!\otimes\!\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}F}}\text{\makebox[14.5pt][c]{$\hskip-150.0pt\displaystyle\text{\makebox[0.0pt][r]{$$}}=\text{\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\raisebox{0.47pt}{\hskip-4.0pt:\hskip 1.25pt}$}}\hskip-150.0pt$}}\bigoplus_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}i}}\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}q}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}i}}
for simple Lie algebras
\mathfrak{g}
except
\mathfrak{e}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}8}}
, which is discussed separately.

\begin{array}[]{|l@{$\,$}|ccc|}\hfil\hfil\lx@intercol&\text{\makebox[40.0pt][c]{$\hskip-150.0pt\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}\mathbf{q}_{1}}\hskip-150.0pt$}}&\text{\makebox[70.0pt][c]{$\hskip-150.0pt\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}\mathbf{q}_{2}}\hskip-150.0pt$}}&\lx@intercol\hfil\text{\makebox[60.0pt][c]{$\hskip-150.0pt\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}\mathbf{q}_{3}}\hskip-150.0pt$}}\hfil\lx@intercol\\

(4.29)

For the classical Lie algebras, some care is required to specify these representations—as there are frequently distinct, irreducible representations that share the same dimension. For the sake of clarity, therefore we have listed both the dimension formulae and the (large-
{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}k}
) structure of these representations’ Dynkin labels (with respect to the conventions outlined in
appendix
B
). (The precise representations’ Dynkin labels may differ from the patterns suggested in
Table
1
in cases of low rank.)

With what can we compare these tensors? A more familiar list of tensors—which in fact always span the space of possible colour tensors—would include the following:

\big\{\mathbf{Q}^{1},\ldots,\mathbf{Q}^{4}\big\}\;\raisebox{-0.95pt}{\scalebox{1.25}{$\Leftrightarrow$}}\;\Bigg\{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{figures/ffff_tensors_a}}}$},\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{figures/fffff_tensors_b}}}$},\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{figures/ffff_tensors_c}}}$},\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{figures/ffff_tensors_d}}}$}\Bigg\}\,;\vskip-0.5pt

(4.30)

and, if the fundamental representation is self-conjugate (if
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}F}}\!\simeq\!\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}\overline{F}}}
) one may also include the tensor

\text{\makebox[0.0pt][r]{$\mathbf{Q}^{5}\;\raisebox{-0.95pt}{\scalebox{1.25}{$\Leftrightarrow$}}\;$}}\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{figures/ffff_tensors_e}}}$}\,.\vskip-0.5pt

(4.31)

By directly constructing each of the colour tensors in the basis (
4.27
), we have determined the coefficients appearing in the expansion

\mathbf{Q}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}i}}\text{\makebox[14.5pt][c]{$\hskip-150.0pt\displaystyle\text{\makebox[0.0pt][r]{$$}}=\text{\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\raisebox{0.47pt}{\hskip-4.0pt:\hskip 1.25pt}$}}\hskip-150.0pt$}}\,\sum_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}j}}\mathbf{c}[\mathfrak{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}g}}]^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}i}}_{\phantom{i}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}j}}\mathcal{B}^{\hskip 1.0pt{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}\mathbf{q}_{j}}}\,.\vskip-6.0pt\vskip-0.5pt

(4.32)

for all of the simple Lie algebras. The results of this computation are documented in
Table
2
.

\text{\makebox[0.0pt][r]{$\text{-}$}}\frac{2{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}k}\hskip 0.0pt{-}\hskip 0.0pt1}{4{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}k}}&\frac{1}{2}&\frac{1}{2}\\[-0.5pt]
\frac{1}{2{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}k}}&1&1\\[-0.5pt]
\frac{1}{2{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}k}}&\text{\makebox[0.0pt][r]{$\text{-}$}}1&1\\[-0.5pt]
1&{\color[rgb]{0.55,0.55,0.55}0}&{\color[rgb]{0.55,0.55,0.55}0}\end{array}\right)\end{array}&\begin{array}[t]{c}\mathfrak{e}_{\text{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{${\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}6}$}}}\\
\left(\begin{array}[]{c@{\;\;\;}c@{\;\;\;}c}\text{\makebox[0.0pt][r]{$\text{-}$}}13&1&2\\[-0.5pt]
\text{\makebox[0.0pt][r]{$\text{-}$}}13&\text{\makebox[0.0pt][r]{$\text{-}$}}1&2\\[-0.5pt]
9&\text{\makebox[0.0pt][r]{$\text{-}$}}9&9\\[-0.5pt]
9&9&9\end{array}\right)\end{array}\end{array}\\
\begin{array}[t]{@{$\!$}c@{$\!$}c@{$\!$}c@{$\!$}c@{$\!$}}\begin{array}[t]{c}\mathfrak{e}_{\text{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{${\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}7}$}}}\\
\left(\begin{array}[]{c@{\;\;\;}c@{\;\;\;}c@{\;\;\;}c}\text{\makebox[0.0pt][r]{$\text{-}$}}\frac{19}{448}&\text{\makebox[0.0pt][r]{$\text{-}$}}7&1&1\\[-0.5pt]
\frac{19}{448}&\text{\makebox[0.0pt][r]{$\text{-}$}}7&\text{\makebox[0.0pt][r]{$\text{-}$}}1&1\\[-0.5pt]
\frac{1}{56}&8&\text{\makebox[0.0pt][r]{$\text{-}$}}24&8\\[-0.5pt]
\text{\makebox[0.0pt][r]{$\text{-}$}}\frac{1}{56}&8&24&8\\[-0.5pt]
1&{\color[rgb]{0.55,0.55,0.55}0}&{\color[rgb]{0.55,0.55,0.55}0}&{\color[rgb]{0.55,0.55,0.55}0}\end{array}\right)\end{array}&\begin{array}[t]{c}\mathfrak{e}_{\text{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{${\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}8}$}}}\\
\left(\begin{array}[]{c@{\;\;\;}c@{\;\;\;}c@{\;\;\;}c@{\;\;\;}c}\text{\makebox[0.0pt][r]{$\text{-}$}}\frac{1}{248}&\text{\makebox[0.0pt][r]{$\text{-}$}}\frac{1}{2}&\text{\makebox[0.0pt][r]{$\text{-}$}}1&{\color[rgb]{0.55,0.55,0.55}0}&1\\[-0.5pt]
\text{\makebox[0.0pt][r]{$\text{-}$}}\frac{1}{248}&\frac{1}{2}&\text{\makebox[0.0pt][r]{$\text{-}$}}1&{\color[rgb]{0.55,0.55,0.55}0}&1\\[-0.5pt]
\frac{1}{248}&1&5&\text{\makebox[0.0pt][r]{$\text{-}$}}1&30\\[-0.5pt]
\frac{1}{248}&\text{\makebox[0.0pt][r]{$\text{-}$}}1&5&1&30\\[-0.5pt]
1&{\color[rgb]{0.55,0.55,0.55}0}&{\color[rgb]{0.55,0.55,0.55}0}&{\color[rgb]{0.55,0.55,0.55}0}&{\color[rgb]{0.55,0.55,0.55}0}\end{array}\right)\end{array}&\begin{array}[t]{c}\mathfrak{f}_{\text{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{${\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}4}$}}}\\
\left(\begin{array}[]{c@{\;\;\;}c@{\;\;\;}c@{\;\;\;}c@{\;\;\;}c}\text{\makebox[0.0pt][r]{$\text{-}$}}\frac{1}{13}&\text{\makebox[0.0pt][r]{$\text{-}$}}1&1&{\color[rgb]{0.55,0.55,0.55}0}&\text{\makebox[0.0pt][r]{$\text{-}$}}1\\[-0.5pt]
\text{\makebox[0.0pt][r]{$\text{-}$}}\frac{1}{13}&1&1&{\color[rgb]{0.55,0.55,0.55}0}&\text{\makebox[0.0pt][r]{$\text{-}$}}1\\[-0.5pt]
\frac{1}{26}&2&6&\text{\makebox[0.0pt][r]{$\text{-}$}}1&1\\[-0.5pt]
\frac{1}{26}&\text{\makebox[0.0pt][r]{$\text{-}$}}2&6&1&1\\[-0.5pt]
1&{\color[rgb]{0.55,0.55,0.55}0}&{\color[rgb]{0.55,0.55,0.55}0}&{\color[rgb]{0.55,0.55,0.55}0}&{\color[rgb]{0.55,0.55,0.55}0}\end{array}\right)\end{array}&\begin{array}[t]{c}\mathfrak{g}_{\text{\makebox[2.0pt][l]{${\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}2}$}}}\\
\left(\begin{array}[]{c@{\;\;\;}c@{\;\;\;}c@{\;\;\;}c}\text{\makebox[0.0pt][r]{$\text{-}$}}\frac{2}{7}&{\color[rgb]{0.55,0.55,0.55}0}&1&1\\[-0.5pt]
\text{\makebox[0.0pt][r]{$\text{-}$}}\frac{2}{7}&{\color[rgb]{0.55,0.55,0.55}0}&1&\text{\makebox[0.0pt][r]{$\text{-}$}}1\\[-0.5pt]
\frac{1}{7}&1&3&\text{\makebox[0.0pt][r]{$\text{-}$}}1\\[-0.5pt]
\frac{1}{7}&\text{\makebox[0.0pt][r]{$\text{-}$}}1&3&1\\[-0.5pt]
1&{\color[rgb]{0.55,0.55,0.55}0}&{\color[rgb]{0.55,0.55,0.55}0}&{\color[rgb]{0.55,0.55,0.55}0}\end{array}\right)\end{array}\end{array}\end{array}

Notice that not all the colour tensors
\mathbf{Q}^{i}
described above are in fact linearly independent. For
\mathfrak{a}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}k}}
, for example, we may construct the ‘null space’ of the coefficients given in
Table
2
to discover the familiar
Fierz identities
:

\begin{split}\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{figures/ffff_tensors_a}}}$}&=\phantom{\frac{1}{2}}\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{figures/ffff_tensors_d}}}$}-\text{\makebox[24.0pt][c]{$\hskip-150.0pt\displaystyle\frac{1}{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}k}{+}1}\hskip-150.0pt$}}\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{figures/ffff_tensors_c}}}$}\\[-10.0pt]
\text{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\hskip-150.0pt\displaystyle\text{\makebox[297.0pt][l]{$(\mathfrak{a}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}k}})$}}\hskip-150.0pt$}}\\[-10.0pt]
\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{figures/fffff_tensors_b}}}$}&=\phantom{\frac{1}{2}}\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{figures/ffff_tensors_c}}}$}-\text{\makebox[24.0pt][c]{$\hskip-150.0pt\displaystyle\frac{1}{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}k}{+}1}\hskip-150.0pt$}}\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{figures/ffff_tensors_d}}}$}\end{split}\vskip-0.5pt

(4.33)

These relations are especially useful in the context of using multi-trace (over the fundamental representation) tensors to encode the colour-dependence of all-adjoint scattering. Upon expanding the colour-dependence of a Feynman diagram into multi-traces, one generically encounters products of traces involving some number of internal ‘linkages’ (including self-linkages). The Fierz identities allow us to expand any such object into multi-trace tensors without such internal linkages via

(\mathfrak{a}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}k}})
\hskip-150.0pt\displaystyle\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{figures/fierz_unwind_a.pdf}}}$}=\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{figures/fierz_unwind_b}}}$}-\frac{1}{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}k}{+}1}\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{figures/fierz_unwind_c.pdf}}}$}\,.\hskip-150.0pt

(4.34)

The above identity shows that any
{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}f}
-graph (in
\mathfrak{a}
-type gauge theory), initially expressed in terms of (linked) products of traces involving via fundamental representation using (
2.31
), can be systematically ‘unwound’ into products of traces only involving external edges. Importantly, all ‘multi-trace’ tensors that result from this will be sub-leading in
{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}k}
. Therefore, in the limit of large
{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}k}
, only single-trace colour tensors contribute to
\mathfrak{a}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}k}}
gauge theory.

Such identities also exist for
\mathfrak{b},\mathfrak{c},\mathfrak{d}
-type Lie algebras. In particular, we can detect the linear dependence among the five
\mathbf{Q}^{i}
using the explicit decompositions given in
Table
2
:

\begin{split}\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{figures/ffff_tensors_a}}}$}&=\frac{1}{2}\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{figures/ffff_tensors_d}}}$}-\text{\makebox[24.0pt][c]{$\hskip-150.0pt\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\hskip-150.0pt$}}\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{figures/ffff_tensors_e}}}$}\\[-10.0pt]
\text{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\hskip-150.0pt\displaystyle\text{\makebox[297.0pt][l]{$(\mathfrak{b}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}k}},\mathfrak{d}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}k}})$}}\hskip-150.0pt$}}\\[-10.0pt]
\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{figures/fffff_tensors_b}}}$}&=\frac{1}{2}\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{figures/ffff_tensors_c}}}$}-\text{\makebox[24.0pt][c]{$\hskip-150.0pt\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\hskip-150.0pt$}}\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{figures/ffff_tensors_e}}}$}\\
\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{figures/ffff_tensors_a}}}$}&=\frac{1}{2}\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{figures/ffff_tensors_d}}}$}-\text{\makebox[24.0pt][c]{$\hskip-150.0pt\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\hskip-150.0pt$}}\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{figures/ffff_tensors_e}}}$}\\[-10.0pt]
\text{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\hskip-150.0pt\displaystyle\text{\makebox[297.0pt][l]{$(\mathfrak{c}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}k}})$}}\hskip-150.0pt$}}\\[-10.0pt]
\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{figures/fffff_tensors_b}}}$}&=\frac{1}{2}\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{figures/ffff_tensors_c}}}$}+\text{\makebox[24.0pt][c]{$\hskip-150.0pt\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\hskip-150.0pt$}}\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{figures/ffff_tensors_e}}}$}\\[-28.0pt]
~\end{split}\vskip-0.5pt

(4.35)

These identities can similarly be used to express any colour tensor arising via the Feynman expansion into the basis of multi-traces (over the fundamental representation) without any internal linkages. In contrast with
\mathfrak{a}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}k}}
gauge theory, however, there is no longer any hierarchy in orders of the rank
{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}k}
of the gauge theory.

Interestingly, this does not appear possible in general for any of the exceptional algebras. Indeed, for
\mathfrak{e}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}8}}
or
\mathfrak{f}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}4}}
gauge theory, there is
no
linear dependence among the tensors
\mathbf{Q}^{i}
; as such, there exists no Fierz-like relation. For the simple Lie algebras
\mathfrak{e}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}6}}
,
\mathfrak{e}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}7}}
, and
\mathfrak{g}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}2}}
, there is linear dependence among the colour tensors
\mathbf{Q}^{i}
, but none of these allow one to systematically eliminate internal linkages among products of traces analogous to (
4.34
):

\begin{split}~\\[-27.0pt]
\text{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\hskip-150.0pt\displaystyle\text{\makebox[438.0pt][l]{$(\mathfrak{e}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}6}})$}}\hskip-150.0pt$}}\text{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\hskip-150.0pt\displaystyle\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{figures/ffff_tensors_a}}}$}=\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{figures/fffff_tensors_b}}}$}-\frac{1}{9}\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{figures/ffff_tensors_c}}}$}+\frac{1}{9}\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{figures/ffff_tensors_d}}}$}\hskip-150.0pt$}}\\
\text{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\hskip-150.0pt\displaystyle\text{\makebox[438.0pt][l]{$(\mathfrak{e}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}7}})$}}\hskip-150.0pt$}}\text{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\hskip-150.0pt\displaystyle\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{figures/ffff_tensors_a}}}$}\hskip-7.0pt=\hskip-2.0pt\phantom{{-}}\hskip-12.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{figures/fffff_tensors_b}}}$}\hskip-14.0pt-\!\text{\makebox[14.0pt][c]{$\hskip-150.0pt\displaystyle\frac{1}{24}\hskip-150.0pt$}}\hskip-2.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{figures/ffff_tensors_c}}}$}\hskip-14.0pt+\!\text{\makebox[14.0pt][c]{$\hskip-150.0pt\displaystyle\frac{1}{24}\hskip-150.0pt$}}\hskip-2.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{figures/ffff_tensors_d}}}$}\hskip-14.0pt-\!\text{\makebox[14.0pt][c]{$\hskip-150.0pt\displaystyle\frac{1}{12}\hskip-150.0pt$}}\hskip-2.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{figures/ffff_tensors_e}}}$}\hskip-150.0pt$}}\\
\text{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\hskip-150.0pt\displaystyle\text{\makebox[438.0pt][l]{$(\mathfrak{g}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}2}})$}}\hskip-150.0pt$}}\text{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\hskip-150.0pt\displaystyle\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{figures/ffff_tensors_a}}}$}\hskip-7.0pt=\hskip-2.0pt{-}\hskip-12.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{figures/fffff_tensors_b}}}$}\hskip-14.0pt+\!\text{\makebox[14.0pt][c]{$\hskip-150.0pt\displaystyle\frac{1}{3}\hskip-150.0pt$}}\hskip-2.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{figures/ffff_tensors_c}}}$}\hskip-14.0pt+\!\text{\makebox[14.0pt][c]{$\hskip-150.0pt\displaystyle\frac{1}{3}\hskip-150.0pt$}}\hskip-2.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{figures/ffff_tensors_d}}}$}\hskip-14.0pt-\!\text{\makebox[14.0pt][c]{$\hskip-150.0pt\displaystyle\frac{2}{3}\hskip-150.0pt$}}\hskip-2.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{figures/ffff_tensors_e}}}$}\hskip-150.0pt$}}\\[-28.0pt]
~\end{split}\vskip-0.5pt

(4.36)

Importantly, none of these relations allow one to convert products of traces involving internal
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}ad}}
edges into those without such edges. As such, there is no analogous trace-expansion for these theories.

Of course, in choosing the basis for colour tensors, we are neither forced to choose any particular tree nor the ordering of the representations appearing on the leaves. Consider for example an alternate choice of basis dictated by the following graph of Clebsch-Gordan tensors:

\text{\makebox[0.0pt][r]{$\mathcal{C}^{\hskip 1.0pt\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.545,0.7451}r}}}(\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}F}}\,\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}F}}|\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}F}}\,\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}F}})\;\raisebox{-0.95pt}{\scalebox{1.25}{$\Leftrightarrow$}}\;$}}\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{figures/ffff_basis_alt_defined}}}$}\,.\vskip-5.0pt\vskip-0.5pt

(4.37)

Like the basis tensors
\mathcal{B}^{\,\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}q}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}i}}}
described in (
4.27
), these tensors are uniquely labelled by the internal representation
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.545,0.7451}r}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.545,0.7451}i}}
appearing in (
4.37
) and arising via

\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}F}}\!\otimes\!\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}\overline{F}}}\text{\makebox[14.5pt][c]{$\hskip-150.0pt\displaystyle\text{\makebox[0.0pt][r]{$$}}=\text{\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\raisebox{0.47pt}{\hskip-4.0pt:\hskip 1.25pt}$}}\hskip-150.0pt$}}\,\bigoplus_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.545,0.7451}i}}\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.545,0.7451}r}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.545,0.7451}i}}\text{\makebox[14.5pt][c]{$\hskip-150.0pt\displaystyle\text{\makebox[0.0pt][r]{$$}}=\text{\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\raisebox{0.47pt}{\hskip-4.0pt:\hskip 1.25pt}$}}\hskip-150.0pt$}}\mathbf{1}\oplus\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}ad}}\oplus\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.545,0.7451}r}}\bigoplus_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.545,0.7451}i}=4}{\color[rgb]{0,0.545,0.7451}\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.545,0.7451}r}}_{i}}\,.\vskip 0.0pt\vskip-0.5pt

(4.38)

For simple Lie algebras with self-conjugate fundamental representations—namely, all but
\mathfrak{a}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}k}>1}
and
\mathfrak{e}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}6}}
—it is obvious that
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}q}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}i}}=\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.545,0.7451}r}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.545,0.7451}i}}
. For
\mathfrak{a}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}k}}
, no
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.545,0.7451}r}}
appears in (
4.38
), as
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}F}}\!\otimes\!\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}\overline{F}}}\!=\!\mathbf{1}\!\oplus\!\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}ad}}
. For
\mathfrak{e}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}6}}
we define

(\mathfrak{e}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}6}})
\hskip-150.0pt\displaystyle\hbox{\hskip 15.11133pt\hskip-3.61803pt\hbox{$\text{\rule[-5.0pt]{0.0pt}{10.0pt}{\normalsize$\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}F}}$}}$}\hskip-3.61803pt\hskip-15.11133pt\raisebox{-6.0pt}{\hbox{$\rule[-4.0pt]{0.0pt}{10.0pt}\text{{\footnotesize$[100000]$}}$}}\hskip-15.11133pt\hskip 15.11133pt}\!\otimes\!\hbox{\hskip 17.23636pt\hskip-2.5pt\hbox{$\text{\rule[-5.0pt]{0.0pt}{10.0pt}{\normalsize$\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}\overline{F}}}$}}$}\hskip-2.5pt\hskip-17.23636pt\raisebox{-6.0pt}{\hbox{$\rule[-4.0pt]{0.0pt}{10.0pt}\text{{\footnotesize$[0000010]$}}$}}\hskip-17.23636pt\hskip 17.23636pt}=\hbox{\hskip 15.11133pt\hskip-2.87498pt\hbox{$\text{\rule[-5.0pt]{0.0pt}{10.0pt}{\normalsize$\mathbf{1}$}}$}\hskip-2.87498pt\hskip-15.11133pt\raisebox{-6.0pt}{\hbox{$\rule[-4.0pt]{0.0pt}{10.0pt}\text{{\footnotesize$[000000]$}}$}}\hskip-15.11133pt\hskip 15.11133pt}\oplus\hbox{\hskip 15.11133pt\hskip-5.98953pt\hbox{$\text{\rule[-5.0pt]{0.0pt}{10.0pt}{\normalsize$\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}ad}}$}}$}\hskip-5.98953pt\hskip-15.11133pt\raisebox{-6.0pt}{\hbox{$\rule[-4.0pt]{0.0pt}{10.0pt}\text{{\footnotesize$[000001]$}}$}}\hskip-15.11133pt\hskip 15.11133pt}\oplus\hbox{\hskip 15.11133pt\hskip-2.36804pt\hbox{$\text{\rule[-5.0pt]{0.0pt}{10.0pt}{\normalsize$\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.545,0.7451}r}}$}}$}\hskip-2.36804pt\hskip-15.11133pt\raisebox{-6.0pt}{\hbox{$\rule[-4.0pt]{0.0pt}{10.0pt}\text{{\footnotesize$[100010]$}}$}}\hskip-15.11133pt\hskip 15.11133pt}\hskip-150.0pt

(4.39)

where
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.545,0.7451}r}}
is the (unique)
\mathbf{650}
-dimensional irreducible representation of
\mathfrak{e}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}6}}
.

Because
\mathbf{1}
and
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}ad}}
always appear in the decomposition of
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}F}}\!\otimes\!\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}\overline{F}}}
, the Clebsch-Gordan colour tensor basis always include
\{\mathbf{Q}^{1},\mathbf{Q}^{3}\}
among these ‘novel’ colour basis tensors. Nevertheless, we can systematically decompose any colour tensor
C(\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}F}}\,\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}F}}|\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}F}}\,\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}F}})
into this basis. For the ‘obvious’
\mathbf{Q}^{i}
tensors, we can systematically decompose them according to

\mathbf{Q}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}i}}\text{\makebox[14.5pt][c]{$\hskip-150.0pt\displaystyle\text{\makebox[0.0pt][r]{$$}}=\text{\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\raisebox{0.47pt}{\hskip-4.0pt:\hskip 1.25pt}$}}\hskip-150.0pt$}}\,\sum_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.545,0.7451}j}}\widetilde{\mathbf{c}}[\mathfrak{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}g}}]^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}i}}_{\phantom{i}{\color[rgb]{0,0.545,0.7451}j}}\mathcal{C}^{\hskip 1.0pt{\color[rgb]{0,0.545,0.7451}\mathbf{r}_{j}}}\,.\vskip-10.0pt\vskip-0.5pt

(4.40)

These coefficients are given for all simple Lie algebras in
Table
3
.

4.3.2
Clebsch Colour Bases for
C(\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}F}}\,\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}ad}}|\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}ad}}\,\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}F}})

Let us now consider the case of scattering two fundamental-charged particles with two adjoints. We’d like to construct a basis for colour tensors of the form
C(\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}F}}\,\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}ad}}|\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}ad}}\,\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}F}})
. Following our prescription, we can construct such a basis according to the tensors

\text{\makebox[0.0pt][r]{$\mathcal{B}^{\hskip 1.0pt\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}s}}}(\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}F}}\,\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}ad}}|\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}ad}}\,\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}F}})\;\raisebox{-0.95pt}{\scalebox{1.25}{$\Leftrightarrow$}}\;$}}\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{figures/fggf_basis_diagram}}}$}\,.\vskip-5.0pt\vskip-0.5pt

(4.41)

As before,
m{\hskip 0.0pt}^{\smash{\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}F}}\,\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}ad}}}}_{\phantom{\smash{\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}F}}\,\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}ad}}}}\smash{\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}s}}}}\!=\!1
for all irreducible representations
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}s}}
appearing in

\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}F}}\!\otimes\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}{ad}}}\text{\makebox[14.5pt][c]{$\hskip-150.0pt\displaystyle\text{\makebox[0.0pt][r]{$$}}=\text{\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\raisebox{0.47pt}{\hskip-4.0pt:\hskip 1.25pt}$}}\hskip-150.0pt$}}\,\bigoplus_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}i}}\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}s}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}i}}\vskip-10.0pt\vskip-0.5pt

(4.42)

and so every colour tensor is uniquely labelled simply by the irreducible representation
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}s}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}i}}
without any need to identify any multiplicity indices. We define the representations
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}s}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}i}}
that appear for each of the simple Lie algebras in
Table
4
. For
\mathfrak{e}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}8}}
, we have that
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}s}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}i}}\!=\!\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}q}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}i}}
given in (
4.29
). As before, the Dynkin labels listed in the table may require modifications for low rank—
e.g.
for
\mathfrak{a}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}1}}
, ‘
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}s}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}3}}
’ in
Table
4.34
would have dimension 0; this should be understood to mean that, for this case, we only have

(\mathfrak{a}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}1}})
\hskip-150.0pt\displaystyle\hbox{\hskip 4.48618pt\hskip-3.61803pt\hbox{$\text{\rule[-5.0pt]{0.0pt}{10.0pt}{\normalsize$\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}F}}$}}$}\hskip-3.61803pt\hskip-4.48618pt\raisebox{-6.0pt}{\hbox{$\rule[-4.0pt]{0.0pt}{10.0pt}\text{{\footnotesize$[1]$}}$}}\hskip-4.48618pt\hskip 4.48618pt}\!\otimes\hbox{\hskip 5.98953pt\hskip-5.98953pt\hbox{$\text{\rule[-5.0pt]{0.0pt}{10.0pt}{\normalsize$\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}ad}}$}}$}\hskip-5.98953pt\hskip-4.48618pt\raisebox{-6.0pt}{\hbox{$\rule[-4.0pt]{0.0pt}{10.0pt}\text{{\footnotesize$[2]$}}$}}\hskip-4.48618pt\hskip 5.98953pt}\text{\makebox[14.5pt][c]{$\hskip-150.0pt\displaystyle\text{\makebox[0.0pt][r]{$$}}=\text{\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\raisebox{0.47pt}{\hskip-4.0pt:\hskip 1.25pt}$}}\hskip-150.0pt$}}\,\hbox{\hskip 4.5111pt\hskip-4.5111pt\hbox{$\text{\rule[-5.0pt]{0.0pt}{10.0pt}{\normalsize$\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}s}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}1}}$}}$}\hskip-4.5111pt\hskip-4.48618pt\raisebox{-7.5pt}{\hbox{$\rule[-4.0pt]{0.0pt}{10.0pt}\text{{\footnotesize$[1]$}}$}}\hskip-4.48618pt\hskip 4.5111pt}\oplus\hbox{\hskip 4.5111pt\hskip-4.5111pt\hbox{$\text{\rule[-5.0pt]{0.0pt}{10.0pt}{\normalsize$\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}s}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}2}}$}}$}\hskip-4.5111pt\hskip-4.48618pt\raisebox{-7.5pt}{\hbox{$\rule[-4.0pt]{0.0pt}{10.0pt}\text{{\footnotesize$[3]$}}$}}\hskip-4.48618pt\hskip 4.5111pt}\,.\hskip-150.0pt

Table 4
:
Irreducible representations appearing in the decomposition of the tensor product
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}F}}\!\otimes\!\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}ad}}\text{\makebox[14.5pt][c]{$\hskip-150.0pt\displaystyle\text{\makebox[0.0pt][r]{$$}}=\text{\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\raisebox{0.47pt}{\hskip-4.0pt:\hskip 1.25pt}$}}\hskip-150.0pt$}}\bigoplus_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}i}}\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}s}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}i}}
for simple Lie algebras
\mathfrak{g}
; the case of
\mathfrak{e}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}8}}
is discussed separately.

There are relatively few familiar colour tensors with which we may compare. An obvious set of tensors (which are complete for all simple Lie algebras except
\mathfrak{e}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}8}}
) would be the following:

\big\{\mathbf{S}^{1},\mathbf{S}^{2},\mathbf{S}^{3}\big\}\;\raisebox{-0.95pt}{\scalebox{1.25}{$\Leftrightarrow$}}\;\Bigg\{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{figures/fggf_reference_tensor_1}}}$},\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{figures/fggf_reference_tensor_2}}}$},\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{figures/fggf_reference_tensor_3}}}$}\Bigg\}\,.\vskip-4.0pt\vskip-0.5pt

(4.44)

Notice that
\mathbf{S}^{1}
is always included among the basis tensors
\mathcal{B}^{\hskip 1.0pt\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}s}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}i}}}
of (
4.41
) as
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}F}}\text{\makebox[14.5pt][c]{$\hskip-150.0pt\displaystyle\text{\makebox[0.0pt][r]{$$}}=\text{\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\raisebox{0.47pt}{\hskip-4.0pt:\hskip 1.25pt}$}}\hskip-150.0pt$}}\,\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}s}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}1}}
for each simple Lie algebra. For
\mathfrak{e}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}8}}
, we take
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}s}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}i}}\text{\makebox[14.5pt][c]{$\hskip-150.0pt\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\text{\makebox[0.0pt][r]{$\raisebox{0.47pt}{\hskip 1.25pt:\hskip-4.0pt}$}}=\text{\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$$}}\hskip-150.0pt$}}\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}q}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}i}}
defined in (
4.29
).

As before, we can decompose the more familiar colour tensors
\mathbf{S}^{i}
into this basis according to

\mathbf{S}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}i}}\text{\makebox[14.5pt][c]{$\hskip-150.0pt\displaystyle\text{\makebox[0.0pt][r]{$$}}=\text{\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\raisebox{0.47pt}{\hskip-4.0pt:\hskip 1.25pt}$}}\hskip-150.0pt$}}\,\sum_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}j}}{\mathbf{c}}[\mathfrak{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}g}}]^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}i}}_{\phantom{i}{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}j}}\mathcal{B}^{\hskip 1.0pt{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}\mathbf{s}_{j}}}\,.\vskip-4.0pt\vskip-0.5pt

(4.45)

These coefficients are enumerated in
Table
5
. In most cases the coefficients
\mathbf{c}[{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}\mathfrak{g}}]
are full rank; as such, we may invert them to effectively
define
the new colour tensors in terms of the more familiar ones.

.

Because there are only two independent tensors for
\mathfrak{a}_{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}1}
, we may deduce the linear relation satisfied by the three colour tensors
\mathbf{S}^{i}
:

(\mathfrak{a}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}1}})
\hskip-150.0pt\displaystyle\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{figures/fggf_tensors_a1_1}}}$}+\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{figures/fggf_tensors_a1_2}}}$}=\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{figures/fggf_tensors_a1_3}}}$}\hskip-150.0pt

(4.46)

which reflects the familiar fact that the fundamental representation of
\mathfrak{a}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}1}}

also
may be used to define a Clifford algebra (satisfying
anti
commutation relations).

As we did before, it is worth considering an alternative choice for the arrangement of the representations to define our basis tensors. In particular, we could have chosen

\text{\makebox[0.0pt][r]{$\mathcal{C}_{\hskip 0.0pt\mu}^{\hskip 1.0pt\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.545,0.7451}r}}}(\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}F}}\,\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}ad}}|\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}ad}}\,\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}F}})\;\raisebox{-0.95pt}{\scalebox{1.25}{$\Leftrightarrow$}}\;$}}\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{figures/fggf_alt_basis_diagram}}}$}\,.\vskip-5.0pt\vskip-0.5pt

(4.47)

Here, the representations
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.545,0.7451}r}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.545,0.7451}i}}
are among the same ones defined above, but must
also
appear in the decomposition of
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}ad}}^{\otimes 2}
into irreducible representations

\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}ad}}\!\otimes\!\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}{ad}}}\text{\makebox[14.5pt][c]{$\hskip-150.0pt\displaystyle\text{\makebox[0.0pt][r]{$$}}=\text{\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\raisebox{0.47pt}{\hskip-4.0pt:\hskip 1.25pt}$}}\hskip-150.0pt$}}\,\bigoplus_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}i}}\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}t}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}i}}\,.\vskip-7.0pt\vskip-0.5pt

(4.48)

We enumerate these representations in the following subsection (see
Table
7
), but suffice it to say that only
\mathbf{1}
,
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}ad}}
, and
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.545,0.7451}r}}
(if this exists) defined in (
4.38
) appear also in (
4.48
) (with the exception of
\mathfrak{e}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}8}}
, of course).

For the
\mathfrak{a}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}k}>1}
-type Lie algebras, for which only the representations
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}r}}\!\in\!\{\mathbf{1},\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}ad}}\}
appear in (
4.47
), it is crucial to note that the second vertex requires a multiplicity index, as
m{\hskip 0.0pt}^{\smash{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}\mathbf{ad\,ad}}}}_{\phantom{\smash{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}\mathbf{ad\,ad}}}}\smash{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}\mathbf{ad}}}}\!=\!2
—reflecting the fact that a non-vanishing
{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}d}
tensor exists (see (
2.56
)). Thus, the basis consists of the three tensors

(\mathfrak{a}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}k}>1})
\hskip-150.0pt\displaystyle\text{\makebox[0.0pt][r]{$\big\{\mathcal{C}_{\hskip 0.0pt\mu}^{\hskip 1.0pt\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.545,0.7451}r}}}\big\}\;\raisebox{-0.95pt}{\scalebox{1.25}{$\Leftrightarrow$}}\;$}}\left\{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{figures/fggf_a_series_alt_basis_1}}}$},\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{figures/fggf_a_series_alt_basis_2}}}$},\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{figures/fggf_a_series_alt_basis_3}}}$}\right\}\,.\hskip-150.0pt

(4.49)

For
\mathfrak{a}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}1}}
, the third tensor above vanishes, leaving us with a basis spanned by the first two. For all other algebras (besides
\mathfrak{e}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}8}}
,) we have

(\hskip-1.25pt\not{\hskip-2.0pt\mathfrak{a}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}k}})}
\hskip-150.0pt\displaystyle\text{\makebox[0.0pt][r]{$\big\{\mathcal{C}_{\hskip 0.0pt\mu}^{\hskip 1.0pt\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.545,0.7451}r}}}\big\}\;\raisebox{-0.95pt}{\scalebox{1.25}{$\Leftrightarrow$}}\;$}}\left\{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{figures/fggf_a_series_alt_basis_1}}}$},\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{figures/fggf_a_series_alt_basis_2}}}$},\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{figures/fggf_nota_alt_basis_3}}}$}\right\}\hskip-150.0pt

(4.50)

where
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.545,0.7451}r}}
is defined in (
4.38
) and is identified with
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}q}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}3}}
in
Table
1
and in (
4.39
) for
\mathfrak{e}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}6}}
.

As before, we can determine the coefficients of the tensors
\mathbf{S}^{i}
in this basis:

\mathbf{S}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}i}}\text{\makebox[14.5pt][c]{$\hskip-150.0pt\displaystyle\text{\makebox[0.0pt][r]{$$}}=\text{\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\raisebox{0.47pt}{\hskip-4.0pt:\hskip 1.25pt}$}}\hskip-150.0pt$}}\,\sum_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.545,0.7451}j}}\widetilde{\mathbf{c}}[\mathfrak{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}g}}]^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}i}}_{\phantom{i}{\color[rgb]{0,0.545,0.7451}j}}\mathcal{C}^{\hskip 1.0pt{\color[rgb]{0,0.545,0.7451}j}}\,.\vskip-5.0pt\vskip-0.5pt

(4.51)

We have computed these explicitly for all simple Lie algebras, and have enumerated the results in
Table
6
.

4.3.3
Clebsch Colour Bases for
C(\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}ad}}\,\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}ad}}|\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}ad}}\,\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}ad}})

In the case of four adjoint-coloured particles, the basis can be expressed

\text{\makebox[0.0pt][r]{$\mathcal{B}_{\mu\nu}^{\hskip 4.0pt\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}t}}}(\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}ad}}\,\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}ad}}|\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}ad}}\,\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}ad}})\;\raisebox{-0.95pt}{\scalebox{1.25}{$\Leftrightarrow$}}\;$}}\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{figures/gggg_basis_diagram}}}$}\,.\vskip-5.0pt\vskip-0.5pt

(4.52)

These tensors are uniquely labelled by the irreducible representations
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}t}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}i}}
in

\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}ad}}\!\otimes\!\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}{ad}}}\text{\makebox[14.5pt][c]{$\hskip-150.0pt\displaystyle\text{\makebox[0.0pt][r]{$$}}=\text{\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\raisebox{0.47pt}{\hskip-4.0pt:\hskip 1.25pt}$}}\hskip-150.0pt$}}\,\bigoplus_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}i}}\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}t}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}i}}\text{\makebox[14.5pt][c]{$\hskip-150.0pt\displaystyle\text{\makebox[0.0pt][r]{$$}}=\text{\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\raisebox{0.47pt}{\hskip-4.0pt:\hskip 1.25pt}$}}\hskip-150.0pt$}}\,\mathbf{1}\oplus\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}ad}}^{m}\bigoplus_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}j}=3}\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}t}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}j}}\,,\vskip-5.0pt\vskip-0.5pt

(4.53)

where
m{=}2
for
\mathfrak{a}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}k}>1}
and
m{=}1
for all other simple Lie algebras. We have enumerated the representations
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}t}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}i}}
appearing in (
4.53
) in
Table
7
. As in similar examples above, there is some ambiguity regarding these representations’ Dynkin labels in the cases of low rank; in such cases, the dimension formulae should help to disambiguate each individual irreducible representation appearing in (
4.53
).

Table

. These are the cases of
\mathfrak{a}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}1}}
,
\mathfrak{a}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}2}}
and

(4.54)

In the first two cases, these identifications are consistent with
Table
7
, provided one drops the irreducible representations indicated with negative dimension; in the last case, the representation denoted ‘
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}t}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}6}}
’ for
\mathfrak{d}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}k}}
in
Table
7
would have dimension ‘
\mathbf{70}
’, but should be represented instead by the sum of the two irreducible representations
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}t}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}6}}\!\oplus\!\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}t}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}7}}
given in (
4.54
) above.

Thus, for the
\mathfrak{a}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}k}>2}
type gauge theories, there will be 9 tensors spanned by those in (
4.52
), which we order as follows:

(\mathfrak{a}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}k}>1})
\hskip-150.0pt\displaystyle\left\{\mathcal{B}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}1}},\ldots,\right\}\raisebox{-0.95pt}{\scalebox{1.25}{$\Leftrightarrow$}}\left\{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{figures/gggg_a_series_basis_1}}}$},\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{figures/gggg_a_series_basis_2}}}$},\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{figures/gggg_a_series_basis_3}}}$},\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{figures/gggg_a_series_basis_4}}}$},\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{figures/gggg_a_series_basis_5}}}$},\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{figures/gggg_a_series_basis_6}}}$},\ldots\right\}\!.\hskip-150.0pt

(4.55)

For
\mathfrak{a}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}2}}
gauge theory, there are only
5
representations
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}t}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}i}}
, leading to a basis of 8 tensors; and for
\mathfrak{a}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}1}}
, only 3 basis elements are needed (as the
{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}d}
tensor vanishes for
\mathfrak{a}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}1}}
).

For all other gauge theories, the
{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}d}
tensor vanishes allowing us to uniquely identify each basis tensor in (
4.52
) simply by the representation
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}t}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}i}}
exchanged. This gives us a basis of 6 tensors for the classical algebras (7 tensors for
\mathfrak{d}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}4}}
), and a basis of 5 tensors for the exceptional algebras. We choose to order these in the obvious way:

(\hskip-1.25pt\not{\hskip-2.0pt\mathfrak{a}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}k}})}
\hskip-150.0pt\displaystyle\text{\makebox[0.0pt][r]{$\left\{\mathcal{B}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}1}},\ldots,\right\}\raisebox{-0.95pt}{\scalebox{1.25}{$\Leftrightarrow$}}$}}\left\{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{figures/gggg_basis_1}}}$},\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{figures/gggg_basis_2}}}$},\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{figures/gggg_basis_3}}}$},\ldots\right\}\!.\hskip-150.0pt

(4.56)

With what tensors should we compare those defined in (
4.52
)? A natural set of familiar tensors would be the multi-traces involving the fundamental representation. Only for
\mathfrak{a}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}k}>1}
gauge theories are the four-particle multi-traces non-dihedrally invariant—because
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}F}}\!\nsim\!{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}\overline{\mathbf{F}}}
.
4
4

4

Of course, the fundamental
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}27}}\!\text{\makebox[14.5pt][c]{$\hskip-150.0pt\displaystyle\text{\makebox[0.0pt][r]{$$}}=\text{\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\raisebox{0.47pt}{\hskip-4.0pt:\hskip 1.25pt}$}}\hskip-150.0pt$}}\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}F}}
of
\mathfrak{e}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}6}}
is also complex; however, traces involving four or fewer particles are in fact dihedrally symmetric.
Thus, we may consider the ‘reference’ tensors given by

\displaystyle\text{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\hskip-150.0pt\displaystyle\raisebox{-12.0pt}{$\text{\makebox[37.5pt][l]{$(\mathfrak{a}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}k}})$}}$}\hskip-150.0pt$}}\big\{\mathbf{T}^{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}1}},\ldots,\mathbf{T}^{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}9}}\big\}\text{\makebox[14.5pt][c]{$\hskip-150.0pt\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\text{\makebox[0.0pt][r]{$\raisebox{0.47pt}{\hskip 1.25pt:\hskip-4.0pt}$}}=\text{\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$$}}\hskip-150.0pt$}}\big\{

as a reference as opposed to

It is instructive to compare the Clebsch-Gordan basis tensors to the multi-traces by determining the coefficients

\mathbf{T}^{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}i}}\text{\makebox[14.5pt][c]{$\hskip-150.0pt\displaystyle\text{\makebox[0.0pt][r]{$$}}=\text{\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\raisebox{0.47pt}{\hskip-4.0pt:\hskip 1.25pt}$}}\hskip-150.0pt$}}\,\sum_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}j}}{\mathbf{c}}[\mathfrak{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}g}}]^{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}i}}_{\phantom{i}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}j}}\mathcal{B}^{\hskip 1.0pt{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}j}}\,.\vskip-10.0pt\vskip-0.5pt

(4.60)

We have computed these coefficients (by direct construction) and enumerate the results in
Table
8
for the simple Lie algebras with the exception of
\mathfrak{d}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}4}}
, which we discuss momentarily.

For most of the classical Lie algebras, we see that multi-traces (over the fundamental representation) suffice to span the space of all possible colour tensors; for these cases, the coefficients given in
Table
8
are full rank, and can be inverted to effectively define the new colour tensors. For the exceptional algebras, the coefficients are less than full-rank, implying one linear relation satisfied by each set of trace tensors.

There is one simple Lie algebra, however, for which there are
more
Clebsch-Gordan tensors than there are distinct multi-traces involving the fundamental representation:
\mathfrak{d}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}4}}
. In this case, there are 7 basis tensors, but only 6 distinct multi-traces.

\hskip-2.0pt\lx@intercol\hfil 1&{\color[rgb]{0.55,0.55,0.55}0}&{\color[rgb]{0.55,0.55,0.55}0}&{\color[rgb]{0.55,0.55,0.55}0}&{\color[rgb]{0.55,0.55,0.55}0}&{\color[rgb]{0.55,0.55,0.55}0}&{\color[rgb]{0.55,0.55,0.55}0}\\[-0.5pt]

(4.61)

An independent colour tensor could be defined by either

\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}S}}}({\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}1\,2\,3\,4})=\frac{1}{8}\mathcal{B}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}1}}{+}\frac{1}{4}\mathcal{B}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}2}}{+}\frac{3}{16}\mathcal{B}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}6}}\quad\text{or}\quad\mathrm{tr}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}\overline{\mathbf{{S}}}}}({\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}1\,2\,3\,4})=\frac{1}{8}\mathcal{B}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}1}}{+}\frac{1}{4}\mathcal{B}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}2}}{+}\frac{3}{16}\mathcal{B}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}7}}\vskip-5.0pt\vskip-0.5pt

(4.62)

where the spinor representations
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}S}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}\overline{\mathbf{S}}}
would have Dynkin labels
[0001]
and
[0010]
, respectively. While we know (from our discussion of the Fierz identity for
\mathfrak{d}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}k}}
algebras) that all
{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}f}
-graphs are expressible in terms of multi-traces over any representation, it is interesting to note that multi-traces involving a single (fundamental) representation do not suffice to capture all (non-perturbative, or anomalous) possible tensors.

4.4
Novel Colour Tensors for Five Particle Scattering Amplitudes

For five (or more) particles, it is straightforward to construct various Clebsch colour bases, the duality maps between them, and to expand more familiar colour tensors into these bases. For each of the examples discussed below (through six particles), we have done this exercise explicitly. However, the notational complexity involved in labeling bases for even all simple Lie algebras, defining other possible tensors and comparing them, grows considerably. Therefore, in this section and the following, we would like to simply sketch the construction of possible bases in physically interesting cases and discuss some of their most salient features.

In
Table
9
, we list the ranks of spaces spanned by colour tensors involving five particles coloured in the adjoint or fundamental representations for each of the simple Lie algebras.

4.4.1
Clebsch Colour Bases for
C(\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}F}}\,\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}F}}\,\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}ad}}|\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}F}}\,\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}F}})

For the scattering of four fundamental-charged particles with one adjoint, one choice of Clebsch colour tensors would be given by

\mathcal{B}^{\hskip 1.0pt\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}q}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}i}}\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}q}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}j}}}(\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}F}}\,\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}F}}\,\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}ad}}|\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}F}}\,\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}F}})\;\raisebox{-0.95pt}{\scalebox{1.25}{$\Leftrightarrow$}}\;

(4.63)

spanned by all the possible representations
({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}\mathbf{q}_{i}\,\mathbf{q}_{j}})
appearing in the decomposition of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}\mathbf{F}}\!\otimes\!\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}F}}
defined in
Table
1
for which
\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}q}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}j}}\!\in\!\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}q}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}i}}\!\otimes\!\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}ad}}
. None of these Clebsch-Gordan coefficients have multiplicity greater than 1, and so no discrete labels are required on any of the vertices. The possible pairs which span the space of colour tensors for each of the simple Lie algebras is given in

It is straightforward to compute the duality matrices

\mathcal{C}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0.545,0.7451}i}}\text{\makebox[14.5pt][c]{$\hskip-150.0pt\displaystyle\text{\makebox[0.0pt][r]{$$}}=\text{\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\raisebox{0.47pt}{\hskip-4.0pt:\hskip 1.25pt}$}}\hskip-150.0pt$}}\sum_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}j}}\mathbf{c}[\mathfrak{g}]{\hskip 0.0pt}^{\smash{{\color[rgb]{0,0.545,0.7451}i}}}_{\phantom{\smash{{\color[rgb]{0,0.545,0.7451}i}}}\smash{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}j}}}\mathcal{B}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}j}}\,.\vskip-0.5pt

(4.65)

For
\mathfrak{a}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}1}}
, we find

\text{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\hskip-150.0pt\displaystyle\text{\makebox[567.0pt][l]{$(\hskip-1.25pt\mathfrak{a}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}1}})$}}\hskip-150.0pt$}}\hskip-100.0pt\text{\makebox[0.0pt][r]{$\mathbf{c}[\mathfrak{a}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}1}}]=$}}\left(\begin{array}[]{c@{$\;\;$}c@{$\;\;$}c}\frac{1}{2}&\frac{1}{2}&\text{\makebox[0.0pt][r]{$\text{-}$}}\frac{1}{2}\\[-1.0pt]
\frac{3}{4}&\text{\makebox[0.0pt][r]{$\text{-}$}}\frac{1}{4}&\frac{1}{4}\\[-1.0pt]
{\color[rgb]{0.55,0.55,0.55}0}&\frac{2}{3}&\frac{1}{3}\end{array}\right).\hskip-100.0pt\vskip-0.5pt

(4.66)

4.4.2
Clebsch Colour Bases for
C(\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}F}}\,\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}ad}}\,\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}ad}}|\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}ad}}\,\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}F}})

For two fundamental-charged particles and three adjoints, we may consider the basis of colour tensors generated by

\text{\makebox[0.0pt][r]{$\mathcal{B}_{\hskip 3.5pt\mu}^{\hskip 1.0pt\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}s}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}i}}\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}s}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}j}}}(\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}F}}\,\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}ad}}\,\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}ad}}|\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}ad}}\,\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}F}})\;\raisebox{-0.95pt}{\scalebox{1.25}{$\Leftrightarrow$}}\;$}}\raisebox{5.25pt}{$\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{figures/fgggf_basis_diagram}}}$}.\vskip-0.5pt

(4.67)

These tensors sometimes involve a multiplicity index
\mu\!\in\![m{\hskip 0.0pt}^{\smash{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}\mathbf{s}_{i}}\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}ad}}}}_{\phantom{\smash{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}\mathbf{s}_{i}}\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}ad}}}}\smash{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}\mathbf{s}_{j}}}}]
. The labels which generate the basis for each of the simple Lie algebras are enumerated in
Table
12
.

\mathfrak{e}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}6}},\mathfrak{e}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}7}},\mathfrak{f}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}4}},\mathfrak{g}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}2}}\end{array}$}}$}&\text{\makebox[5.0pt][r]{$\big\{\!$}}\rule{0.0pt}{14.0pt}(\hskip-1.0pt{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}\mathbf{s}_{1}}\hskip 1.0pt{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}\mathbf{s}_{1}}\hskip-1.0pt)\text{\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\hskip-1.0pt,$}}&(\hskip-1.0pt{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}\mathbf{s}_{1}}\hskip 1.0pt{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}\mathbf{s}_{2}}\hskip-1.0pt)\text{\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\hskip-1.0pt,$}}&(\hskip-1.0pt{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}\mathbf{s}_{1}}\hskip 1.0pt{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}\mathbf{s}_{3}}\hskip-1.0pt)\text{\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\hskip-1.0pt,$}}&(\hskip-1.0pt{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}\mathbf{s}_{2}}\hskip 1.0pt{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}\mathbf{s}_{1}}\hskip-1.0pt)\text{\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\hskip-1.0pt,$}}&(\hskip-1.0pt{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}\mathbf{s}_{2}}\hskip 1.0pt{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}\mathbf{s}_{2}}\hskip-1.0pt)\text{\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\hskip-1.0pt,$}}&\hbox{\multirowsetup$\text{\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\hskip 4.0pt10$}}$}\\
&&(\hskip-1.0pt{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}\mathbf{s}_{2}}\hskip 1.0pt{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}\mathbf{s}_{3}}\hskip-1.0pt)\text{\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\hskip-1.0pt,$}}&(\hskip-1.0pt{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}\mathbf{s}_{3}}\hskip 1.0pt{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}\mathbf{s}_{1}}\hskip-1.0pt)\text{\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\hskip-1.0pt,$}}&(\hskip-1.0pt{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}\mathbf{s}_{3}}\hskip 1.0pt{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}\mathbf{s}_{2}}\hskip-1.0pt)\text{\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\hskip-1.0pt,$}}&(\hskip-1.0pt{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}\mathbf{s}_{3}}\hskip 1.0pt{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}\mathbf{s}_{3}}\hskip-1.0pt)\text{\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\hskip 0.0pt{}_{\mu\in[2]}$}}\text{\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\hskip 19.0pt\big\}$}}&\\[2.0pt]
\cline{3-7}\cr\end{array}\hskip-200.0pt

Alternatively, one could consider a basis of Clebsch colour tensors generated by the tree graph:

\text{\makebox[0.0pt][r]{$\mathcal{C}_{\hskip 2.5pt\mu\nu}^{\hskip 1.0pt\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.545,0.7451}r}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.545,0.7451}i}}\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}t}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}j}}}(\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}F}}\,\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}ad}}\,\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}ad}}|\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}ad}}\,\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}F}})\;\raisebox{-0.95pt}{\scalebox{1.25}{$\Leftrightarrow$}}\;$}}\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{figures/fgggf_alt_basis_diagram}}}$}\,.\vskip-2.5pt\vskip-0.5pt

(4.68)

These colour tensors are generally labelled by both the pair of irreducible representations
({\color[rgb]{0,0.545,0.7451}\mathbf{r}_{i}}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}\mathbf{t}_{j}})
, with
{\color[rgb]{0,0.545,0.7451}\mathbf{r}_{i}}
defined in (
4.38
) and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}\mathbf{t}_{j}}
enumerated for the simple Lie algebras in
Table
7
, and also the multiplicity indices
\mu\!\in\![m{\hskip 0.0pt}^{\smash{{\color[rgb]{0,0.545,0.7451}\mathbf{r}_{i}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}\mathbf{ad}}}}_{\phantom{\smash{{\color[rgb]{0,0.545,0.7451}\mathbf{r}_{i}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}\mathbf{ad}}}}\smash{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}\mathbf{t}_{j}}}}]
and
\nu\!\in\![m{\hskip 0.0pt}^{\smash{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}\mathbf{t}_{j}}\,\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}ad}}}}_{\phantom{\smash{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}\mathbf{t}_{j}}\,\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}ad}}}}\smash{\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}ad}}}}]
. While it would be somewhat cumbersome to enumerate all the label sets for the
\mathcal{C}
-basis in general, for
\mathfrak{a}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}1}}
, these are given by:

(\hskip-1.25pt\mathfrak{a}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}1}})
\hskip-150.0pt\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{c}\rule{0.0pt}{14.0pt}\big\{(\hskip-1.0pt\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.545,0.7451}1}}\hskip 0.0pt\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}3}}\hskip-1.0pt),(\hskip-1.0pt\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.545,0.7451}3}}\hskip 0.0pt\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}1}}\hskip-1.0pt),(\hskip-1.0pt\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.545,0.7451}3}}\hskip 0.0pt\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}3}}\hskip-1.0pt),(\hskip-1.0pt\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.545,0.7451}3}}\hskip 0.0pt\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}5}}\hskip-1.0pt)\big\}\text{\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\,.$}}\end{array}\hskip-150.0pt

(4.69)

It is interesting to consider the duality matrices

\mathcal{C}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0.545,0.7451}i}}\text{\makebox[14.5pt][c]{$\hskip-150.0pt\displaystyle\text{\makebox[0.0pt][r]{$$}}=\text{\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\raisebox{0.47pt}{\hskip-4.0pt:\hskip 1.25pt}$}}\hskip-150.0pt$}}\sum_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}j}}\mathbf{c}[\mathfrak{g}]{\hskip 0.0pt}^{\smash{{\color[rgb]{0,0.545,0.7451}i}}}_{\phantom{\smash{{\color[rgb]{0,0.545,0.7451}i}}}\smash{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}j}}}\mathcal{B}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}j}}\,.\vskip-0.5pt

(4.70)

For
\mathfrak{a}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}1}}
, we find

\text{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\hskip-150.0pt\displaystyle\text{\makebox[550.0pt][l]{$(\hskip-1.25pt\mathfrak{a}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}1}})$}}\hskip-150.0pt$}}\hskip-100.0pt\text{\makebox[0.0pt][r]{$\mathbf{c}[\mathfrak{a}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}1}}]=$}}\displaystyle\left(\begin{array}[]{c@{$\;\;$}c@{$\;\;$}c@{$\;\;$}c}\text{\makebox[0.0pt][r]{$\text{-}$}}\frac{8}{9}&\frac{2}{3}&\frac{2}{3}&\text{\makebox[0.0pt][r]{$\text{-}$}}\frac{2}{3}\\[-1.0pt]
\frac{2}{3}&1&{\color[rgb]{0.55,0.55,0.55}0}&{\color[rgb]{0.55,0.55,0.55}0}\\[-1.0pt]
\frac{16}{9}&\text{\makebox[0.0pt][r]{$\text{-}$}}\frac{4}{3}&\frac{2}{3}&\text{\makebox[0.0pt][r]{$\text{-}$}}\frac{2}{3}\\[-1.0pt]
{\color[rgb]{0.55,0.55,0.55}0}&{\color[rgb]{0.55,0.55,0.55}0}&\frac{5}{6}&\frac{1}{6}\end{array}\right).\hskip-100.0pt\vskip-0.5pt

(4.71)

4.4.3
Clebsch Colour Bases for
C(\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}ad}}\,\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}ad}}\,\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}ad}}|\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}ad}}\,\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}ad}})

For the scattering of 5 adjoint-coloured particles, one may consider the basis of Clebsch colour tensors given by

\text{\makebox[0.0pt][r]{$\mathcal{B}^{\hskip 2.0pt\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}t}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}i}}\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}t}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}j}}}_{\mu\nu\rho}(\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}ad}}\,\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}ad}}\,\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}ad}}|\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}ad}}\,\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}ad}})\;\raisebox{-0.95pt}{\scalebox{1.25}{$\Leftrightarrow$}}\;$}}\raisebox{5.25pt}{$\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{figures/ggggg_basis_digram}}}$}\,.\vskip-0.5pt

(4.72)

These are labelled both by a pair of irreducible representations
({\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}\mathbf{t}_{i}\,\mathbf{t}_{j}})
defined in
Table
7
and also multiplicity indices
\mu\!\in\![m{\hskip 0.0pt}^{\smash{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}\mathbf{ad\,ad}}}}_{\phantom{\smash{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}\mathbf{ad\,ad}}}}\smash{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}\mathbf{t}_{i}}}}]
,
\nu\!\in\![m{\hskip 0.0pt}^{\smash{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}\mathbf{t}_{i}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}\mathbf{ad}}}}_{\phantom{\smash{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}\mathbf{t}_{i}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}\mathbf{ad}}}}\smash{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}\mathbf{t}_{j}}}}]
, and
\rho\!\in\![m{\hskip 0.0pt}^{\smash{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}\mathbf{t}_{j}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}\mathbf{ad}}}}_{\phantom{\smash{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}\mathbf{t}_{j}}\,{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}\mathbf{ad}}}}\smash{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}\mathbf{ad}}}}]
. Conveniently, for most simple Lie algebras, few multiplicity indices are actually required. The complete list of these (according to the indexing given in
Table
7
) are listed in
Table
13

For
\mathfrak{a}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}1}}
gauge theory, we may compare this basis to one generated by independent multi-trace tensors
\{\mathbf{T}_{1},\ldots,\mathbf{T}_{6}\}
given by

(\hskip-1.25pt\mathfrak{a}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}1}})
\hskip-150.0pt\displaystyle\hskip-10.0pt\Big\{\text{\makebox[55.0pt][l]{$\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}F}}}(\hskip-1.0pt{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}12345})$}},\text{\makebox[55.0pt][l]{$\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}F}}}(\hskip-1.0pt{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}12}|{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}345})$}},\text{\makebox[55.0pt][l]{$\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}F}}}(\hskip-1.0pt{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}23}|{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}451})$}},\text{\makebox[55.0pt][l]{$\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}F}}}(\hskip-1.0pt{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}34}|{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}512})$}},\text{\makebox[55.0pt][l]{$\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}F}}}(\hskip-1.0pt{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}45}|{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}123})$}},\text{\makebox[55.0pt][l]{$\mathrm{tr}_{\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}F}}}(\hskip-1.0pt{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}51}|{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}234})$}}\,\Big\}\text{\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$.$}}\hskip-150.0pt

(4.73)

It is easy to see that these are far from diagonal in colour by computing the overlap

\text{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\hskip-150.0pt\displaystyle\text{\makebox[510.0pt][l]{$(\hskip-1.25pt\mathfrak{a}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}1}})$}}\hskip-150.0pt$}}\hskip-100.0pt\text{\makebox[0.0pt][r]{$\langle\mathbf{T}_{i}|\mathbf{T}_{j}\rangle=$}}\frac{1}{2}\!\left(\begin{array}[]{c@{$\;$}c@{$\;$}c@{$\;$}c@{$\;$}c@{$\;$}c}15&9&9&9&9&9\\[-4.0pt]
9&{\color[rgb]{0.55,0.55,0.55}0}&{\color[rgb]{0.55,0.55,0.55}0}&6&18&6\\[-4.0pt]
9&{\color[rgb]{0.55,0.55,0.55}0}&6&18&6&{\color[rgb]{0.55,0.55,0.55}0}\\[-4.0pt]
9&6&18&6&{\color[rgb]{0.55,0.55,0.55}0}&{\color[rgb]{0.55,0.55,0.55}0}\\[-4.0pt]
9&18&6&{\color[rgb]{0.55,0.55,0.55}0}&{\color[rgb]{0.55,0.55,0.55}0}&6\\[-4.0pt]
9&6&{\color[rgb]{0.55,0.55,0.55}0}&{\color[rgb]{0.55,0.55,0.55}0}&6&18\end{array}\right)\text{\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$.$}}\hskip-100.0pt\vskip-0.5pt

(4.74)

In terms of the basis tensors
\mathcal{B}
, these can be expanded

\mathbf{T}^{i}\text{\makebox[14.5pt][c]{$\hskip-150.0pt\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\text{\makebox[0.0pt][r]{$\raisebox{0.47pt}{\hskip 1.25pt:\hskip-4.0pt}$}}=\text{\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$$}}\hskip-150.0pt$}}\sum_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}j}}\mathbf{c}[\mathfrak{a}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}1}}]{\hskip 0.0pt}^{\smash{i}}_{\phantom{\smash{i}}\smash{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}j}}}\,\mathcal{B}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}j}}\vskip-0.5pt

(4.75)

where the matrix is given by:

\text{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\hskip-150.0pt\displaystyle\text{\makebox[530.0pt][l]{$(\hskip-1.25pt\mathfrak{a}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}1}})$}}\hskip-150.0pt$}}\hskip-100.0pt\text{\makebox[0.0pt][r]{$\mathbf{c}[\mathfrak{a}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}1}}]{\hskip 0.0pt}^{\smash{i}}_{\phantom{\smash{i}}\smash{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}j}}}=$}}\left(\begin{array}[]{c@{$\;\;$}c@{$\;\;$}c@{$\;\;$}c@{$\;\;$}c@{$\;\;$}c}\frac{1}{4}&\frac{1}{4}&\frac{1}{8}&{\color[rgb]{0.55,0.55,0.55}0}&{\color[rgb]{0.55,0.55,0.55}0}&{\color[rgb]{0.55,0.55,0.55}0}\\[-1.0pt]
\frac{1}{2}&{\color[rgb]{0.55,0.55,0.55}0}&{\color[rgb]{0.55,0.55,0.55}0}&{\color[rgb]{0.55,0.55,0.55}0}&{\color[rgb]{0.55,0.55,0.55}0}&{\color[rgb]{0.55,0.55,0.55}0}\\[-1.0pt]
\frac{1}{6}&{\color[rgb]{0.55,0.55,0.55}0}&\frac{1}{8}&{\color[rgb]{0.55,0.55,0.55}0}&\frac{1}{2}&{\color[rgb]{0.55,0.55,0.55}0}\\[-1.0pt]
{\color[rgb]{0.55,0.55,0.55}0}&\frac{1}{6}&\frac{1}{8}&\frac{1}{2}&{\color[rgb]{0.55,0.55,0.55}0}&{\color[rgb]{0.55,0.55,0.55}0}\\[-1.0pt]
{\color[rgb]{0.55,0.55,0.55}0}&\frac{1}{2}&{\color[rgb]{0.55,0.55,0.55}0}&{\color[rgb]{0.55,0.55,0.55}0}&{\color[rgb]{0.55,0.55,0.55}0}&{\color[rgb]{0.55,0.55,0.55}0}\\[-1.0pt]
\frac{1}{6}&\frac{1}{6}&\frac{1}{16}&\text{\makebox[0.0pt][r]{$\text{-}$}}\frac{1}{4}&\text{\makebox[0.0pt][r]{$\text{-}$}}\frac{1}{4}&\frac{1}{4}\end{array}\right)\text{\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$.$}}\hskip-100.0pt\vskip-0.5pt

(4.76)

We can also check the sequence (
3.27
) by comparing the basis
\mathcal{B}
to those of

\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{$\;\;\;$}c@{$\;\;\;$}c@{$\;\;\;$}c@{}}\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{figures/ggggg_alt_basis_4_diagram}}}$}&\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{figures/ggggg_alt_basis_3_diagram}}}$}&\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{figures/ggggg_alt_basis_2_diagram}}}$}&\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\vbox{\hbox{\includegraphics[scale]{figures/ggggg_alt_basis_1_diagram}}}$}\\

(4.77)

where the duality matrix for
\mathfrak{a}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}1}}
is given by

\text{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\hskip-150.0pt\displaystyle\text{\makebox[530.0pt][l]{$(\hskip-1.25pt\mathfrak{a}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}1}})$}}\hskip-150.0pt$}}\hskip-100.0pt\text{\makebox[0.0pt][r]{$\mathbf{d}\text{\makebox[14.5pt][c]{$\hskip-150.0pt\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\text{\makebox[0.0pt][r]{$\raisebox{0.47pt}{\hskip 1.25pt:\hskip-4.0pt}$}}=\text{\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$$}}\hskip-150.0pt$}}$}}\left(\begin{array}[]{c@{$\;\;$}c@{$\;\;$}c@{$\;\;$}c@{$\;\;$}c@{$\;\;$}c}{\color[rgb]{0.55,0.55,0.55}0}&\frac{1}{3}&\frac{1}{4}&1&{\color[rgb]{0.55,0.55,0.55}0}&{\color[rgb]{0.55,0.55,0.55}0}\\[-1.0pt]

(4.78)

for which
\mathbf{d}^{5}{=}\mathbbm{1}_{6}
is a rather non-trivial identity.

4.5
Novel Colour Tensors for Six Particle Scattering Amplitudes

For six external particles transforming in the adjoint or fundamental representations, the number of basis tensors grows much more considerably. The number of independent tensors which exist for each of the simple Lie algebras is given in
Table
14
.

Below, we describe possible Clebsch colour tensor bases in each case, identifying how each basis element would be labelled by irreducible representations and multiplicities. This rapidly becomes too cumbersome to warrant a thorough treatment, but explicit results for
\mathfrak{a}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}1}}
are described in some detail.

Alternatively, one could consider a basis of Clebsch colour tensors generated by the tree graph:

\mathcal{C}_{\hskip 11.5pt\mu}^{\,\smash{\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}q}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}i}}\,\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.545,0.7451}r}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.545,0.7451}j}}\,\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}q}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}k}}}}(\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}F}}\,\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}F}}\,\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}F}}|\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}F}}\,\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}F}}\,\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}F}})\;\raisebox{-0.95pt}{\scalebox{1.25}{$\Leftrightarrow$}}\;

.

(4.80)

(There are of course many inequivalent tree graphs that one could use to define a basis of Clebsch colour tensors.) In the case of
\mathfrak{a}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}1}}
gauge theory, the five independent colour tensors in the
\mathcal{C}
basis would be labelled by

(\hskip-1.25pt\mathfrak{a}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}1}})
\hskip-150.0pt\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{c}\rule{0.0pt}{14.0pt}\big\{(\hskip-1.0pt\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}1}}\hskip 0.0pt\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.545,0.7451}1}}\hskip 0.0pt\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}1}}\hskip-1.0pt),(\hskip-1.0pt\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}1}}\hskip 0.0pt\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.545,0.7451}3}}\hskip 0.0pt\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}3}}\hskip-1.0pt),(\hskip-1.0pt\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}3}}\hskip 0.0pt\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.545,0.7451}1}}\hskip 0.0pt\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}3}}\hskip-1.0pt),(\hskip-1.0pt\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}3}}\hskip 0.0pt\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.545,0.7451}3}}\hskip 0.0pt\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}1}}\hskip-1.0pt),(\hskip-1.0pt\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}3}}\hskip 0.0pt\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.545,0.7451}3}}\hskip 0.0pt\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}3}}\hskip-1.0pt)\big\}\text{\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$\,.$}}\end{array}\hskip-150.0pt

(4.81)

The duality between these basis is encoded by the relation

\mathcal{C}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0.545,0.7451}i}}\text{\makebox[14.5pt][c]{$\hskip-150.0pt\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\text{\makebox[0.0pt][r]{$\raisebox{0.47pt}{\hskip 1.25pt:\hskip-4.0pt}$}}=\text{\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$$}}\hskip-150.0pt$}}\sum_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}j}}\mathbf{c}[\mathfrak{g}]{\hskip 0.0pt}^{\smash{{\color[rgb]{0,0.545,0.7451}i}}}_{\phantom{\smash{{\color[rgb]{0,0.545,0.7451}i}}}\smash{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}j}}}\mathcal{B}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}j}}\,,\vskip-10.0pt\vskip-0.5pt

(4.82)

for which the coefficients can be found to be

\text{\makebox[0.0pt][c]{$\hskip-150.0pt\displaystyle\text{\makebox[550.0pt][l]{$(\hskip-1.25pt\mathfrak{a}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}1}})$}}\hskip-150.0pt$}}\hskip-100.0pt\text{\makebox[0.0pt][r]{$\mathbf{c}[\mathfrak{a}_{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}1}}]{\hskip 0.0pt}^{\smash{{\color[rgb]{0,0.545,0.7451}i}}}_{\phantom{\smash{{\color[rgb]{0,0.545,0.7451}i}}}\smash{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}j}}}=$}}\left(\begin{array}[]{c@{$\;\;$}c@{$\;\;$}c@{$\;\;$}c@{$\;\;$}c}\text{\makebox[0.0pt][r]{$\text{-}$}}1&{\color[rgb]{0.55,0.55,0.55}0}&{\color[rgb]{0.55,0.55,0.55}0}&{\color[rgb]{0.55,0.55,0.55}0}&{\color[rgb]{0.55,0.55,0.55}0}\\[-1.0pt]
{\color[rgb]{0.55,0.55,0.55}0}&\text{\makebox[0.0pt][r]{$\text{-}$}}1&{\color[rgb]{0.55,0.55,0.55}0}&{\color[rgb]{0.55,0.55,0.55}0}&{\color[rgb]{0.55,0.55,0.55}0}\\[-1.0pt]
{\color[rgb]{0.55,0.55,0.55}0}&{\color[rgb]{0.55,0.55,0.55}0}&{\color[rgb]{0.55,0.55,0.55}0}&\text{\makebox[0.0pt][r]{$\text{-}$}}\frac{2}{3}&\text{\makebox[0.0pt][r]{$\text{-}$}}1\\[-1.0pt]
{\color[rgb]{0.55,0.55,0.55}0}&{\color[rgb]{0.55,0.55,0.55}0}&\text{\makebox[0.0pt][r]{$\text{-}$}}1&{\color[rgb]{0.55,0.55,0.55}0}&{\color[rgb]{0.55,0.55,0.55}0}\\[-1.0pt]
{\color[rgb]{0.55,0.55,0.55}0}&{\color[rgb]{0.55,0.55,0.55}0}&{\color[rgb]{0.55,0.55,0.55}0}&\frac{4}{3}&\text{\makebox[0.0pt][r]{$\text{-}$}}1\end{array}\right)\text{\makebox[0.0pt][l]{$.$}}\hskip-100.0pt\vskip-0.5pt

(4.83)

4.5.2
Clebsch Colour Bases for
C(\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}F}}\,\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}F}}\,\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}ad}}|\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}ad}}\,\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}F}}\,\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}F}})

For four fundamentals and two adjoints, one may consider the basis of Clebsch colour tensors given by

\mathcal{B}^{\,\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}q}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}i}}\,\mathbf{v}_{j}\,\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}q}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}k}}}(\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}F}}\,\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}F}}\,\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}ad}}|\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}ad}}\,\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}F}}\,\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0.575}F}})\;\raisebox{-0.95pt}{\scalebox{1.25}{$\Leftrightarrow$}}\;

.

(4.84)

These are labelled by the irreducible representations
({\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}\mathbf{q}_{i}}\,\mathbf{v}_{j}\,{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}\mathbf{q}_{k}})
. (For none of the simple Lie algebras do any Clebsch coefficients require multiplicity indices.) The
new
representations
\mathbf{v}_{j}
appearing in the tensor product
{\color[rgb]{0,0.5,0.4}\mathbf{q}_{i}}\!\otimes\!\mathbf{{\color[rgb]{0.575,0,0.225}ad}}
(not already identified) are defined in
Table
17
for each of the simple Lie algebras, and the sets of labels spanning the basis (
4.84
) are given in
Table
18
.
